   29: # File extensions that Database::Abstraction can probe, in probe order.
   30: # .sql, .sqlite, and .sqlite3 are all SQLite databases; .db is Berkeley DB.
   31: Readonly my @SUPPORTED_EXT => qw( csv db sql sqlite sqlite3 xml tsv psv xlsx );
   32: # Use \z (absolute end-of-string) not $ (which permits a trailing \n before \z).
   33: # A query param decoded from "file.csv%0A" has basename "sales.csv\n"; without \z
   34: # that passes the extension guard and reaches realpath with an embedded newline.
   35: Readonly my $EXT_RE        => do { my $p = join '|', @SUPPORTED_EXT; qr/\.(?:$p)\z/i };
   36: 
   37: # Table name safe-identifier pattern -- must match DataSource's TABLE_NAME_RE
   38: # exactly: first character must be a letter or underscore (not a digit), so
   39: # that every table name the controller accepts can also be opened by DataSource
   40: # without an unhandled croak.
   41: Readonly my $TABLE_NAME_RE => qr/\A[A-Za-z_][A-Za-z0-9_]*\z/;
   42: 
   43: # URL spec pattern shared by _open_spec and _spec_to_url.
   44: # qr{} delimiter avoids the \/ escaping noise required by the // form.
   45: # /s: makes . match \n so a percent-decoded newline inside a URL does not
   46: # silently truncate the captured URL before the \z end-of-string anchor.
   47: Readonly my $URL_SPEC_RE   => qr{\Aurl:(https?://.+)\z}si;
   48: 
   49: # Redis spec pattern: redis:tablename|redis://[password@]host[:port][/db_index]
   50: # The pipe separates the table name from the connection URL so the URL's own
   51: # colons and slashes do not require extra quoting.

   53:

  179: # _is_safe_url($url) -> bool
  180: #
  181: # Purpose: SSRF guard.  Blocks the most dangerous classes of Server-Side Request
  182: #          Forgery targets: loopback aliases (localhost, 127/8) and literal
  183: #          private/link-local/CGNAT IPv4 addresses in the URL host component.
  184: #
  185: # Design rationale for scope:
  186: #   Resolving hostnames via DNS and checking the result is ineffective: a
  187: #   separate DNS lookup happens at LWP connect time (TOCTOU / DNS rebinding
  188: #   window).  The authoritative defence against hostname-based SSRF is a
  189: #   network-layer egress firewall.  This function handles the Perl-layer
  190: #   interception for the most common patterns that operators cannot easily
  191: #   filter at the network level: bare loopback aliases and literal private IPs
  192: #   hard-coded by an attacker.
  193: #

  Mutants (Total: 2, Killed: 2, Survived: 0)
  194: # Blocked targets:
  195: #   localhost / 127.0.0.0/8 / 0.0.0.0 / ::1  -- loopback aliases
  196: #   10.0.0.0/8    -- RFC 1918 private (literal IP)
  197: #   172.16.0.0/12 -- RFC 1918 private (literal IP)

  Mutants (Total: 2, Killed: 2, Survived: 0)
  198: #   192.168.0.0/16-- RFC 1918 private (literal IP)
  199: #   169.254.0.0/16-- link-local; AWS/GCP/Azure metadata endpoint (literal IP)
  200: #   100.64.0.0/10 -- CGNAT / Tailscale shared space (literal IP)
  201: #
  202: # Hostname-based targets (e.g. http://internal.corp.example.com/) are allowed
  203: # at this layer; block them with egress firewall rules instead.
  204: sub _is_safe_url {
  205: 	my $url = $_[0];

  Mutants (Total: 2, Killed: 2, Survived: 0)
  206: 
  207: 	return 0 unless $url =~ m{\Ahttps?://([^/:?\[\]#]+)}i;
  208: 	my $host = lc $1;
  209: 
  210: 	# Block well-known loopback aliases.

  Mutants (Total: 3, Killed: 3, Survived: 0)
  211: 	return 0 if $host eq 'localhost'

  Mutants (Total: 3, Killed: 3, Survived: 0)
  212: 	          || $host =~ /\A127\./

  Mutants (Total: 3, Killed: 3, Survived: 0)
  213: 	          || $host eq '0.0.0.0'

  Mutants (Total: 3, Killed: 3, Survived: 0)
  214: 	          || $host eq '::1';

  Mutants (Total: 3, Killed: 3, Survived: 0)
  215: 

  Mutants (Total: 2, Killed: 2, Survived: 0)
  216: 	# For literal IPv4 addresses only: check private/link-local/CGNAT ranges.
  217: 	# Skipping DNS for hostname targets avoids a live network call in tests and
  218: 	# removes the TOCTOU window that makes DNS-resolved checks illusory anyway.
  219: 	return 1 unless $host =~ /\A\d{1,3}(?:\.\d{1,3}){3}\z/;
  220: 
  221: 	my $packed = inet_aton($host) or return 1;
  222: 	my $n = unpack 'N', $packed;
  223: 
  224: 	return 0 if ($n & 0xFF000000) == 0x0A000000;	# 10/8
  225: 	return 0 if ($n & 0xFFF00000) == 0xAC100000;	# 172.16/12
  226: 	return 0 if ($n & 0xFFFF0000) == 0xC0A80000;	# 192.168/16
  227: 	return 0 if ($n & 0xFFFF0000) == 0xA9FE0000;	# 169.254/16 (link-local / metadata)
  228: 	return 0 if ($n & 0xFFC00000) == 0x64400000;	# 100.64/10 (CGNAT)
  229: 	return 1;
  230: }

  232: # _safe_back_url($url) -> $url | undef

  Mutants (Total: 2, Killed: 2, Survived: 0)
  233: #

  Mutants (Total: 2, Killed: 2, Survived: 0)
  234: # Purpose: Sanitise a user-supplied back-link URL before it is passed to the

  Mutants (Total: 2, Killed: 2, Survived: 0)
  235: #          template and rendered inside an href="" attribute.  Template Toolkit's
  236: #          | html filter encodes <>&" but does NOT block the javascript: or
  237: #          data: URI schemes, so a raw param('back') = "javascript:alert(1)"
  238: #          would be rendered verbatim inside the href and execute on click.
  239: #
  240: # Rule:    Only absolute http(s) URLs and root-relative paths (starting with /)
  241: #          are permitted.  Anything else (javascript:, data:, vbscript:, empty
  242: #          strings, undef) returns undef so the template renders no href.
  243: #
  244: sub _safe_back_url {
  245: 	my ($url) = @_;
  246: 	return undef unless defined $url && length($url);
  247: 	return $url if $url =~ m{\A(?:/(?!/)|https?://)}i;
  248: 	return undef;
  249: }

  473: # _apply_filter_spec($records, $spec) -> \@filtered_records
  474: #
  475: # Purpose: Apply one "col:op:val" filter to an arrayref of record hashrefs and
  476: #          return a new (possibly shorter) arrayref.  The original is not mutated.
  477: #          Value may contain colons: "col:op:val:with:colons" -- split limit 3
  478: #          puts everything after the second colon into $val.
  479: #          Unknown operators fall through to 1 (all rows pass) so new operators
  480: #          added in future do not break existing callers.
  481: # Entry:   $records is an arrayref of hashrefs; $spec is a colon-delimited string.
  482: # Exit:    Returns filtered arrayref (same reference if spec is invalid/unparseable).
  483: #
  484: # Domain Constraints (operator names):
  485: #   Operator names are CASE-SENSITIVE.  Only the exact lowercase forms below are
  486: #   recognised; an uppercase or mixed-case name (e.g. "EQ", "Lt") falls through
  487: #   to the default `1` branch and all rows pass unfiltered.
  488: #   Valid operators:
  489: #     String (value comparison is case-insensitive via lc()):
  490: #       eq, ne, contains, starts
  491: #     Numeric (Perl <, <=, >, >= coercion):
  492: #       lt (strict), le (inclusive), gt (strict), ge (inclusive)
  493: #     Unary (val is ignored):
  494: #       empty (cell eq ''), notempty (cell ne '')
  495: #   Boundary rules for numeric operators:
  496: #     le/ge include the boundary value; lt/gt exclude it.

  Mutants (Total: 2, Killed: 2, Survived: 0)
  497: #   contains/starts with empty val ('') match every record (index always 0).
  498: #
  499: # Reduction: split(/:/, $spec, 3) always produces at least one defined element,
  500: # so "defined $col" is structurally guaranteed true and is removed.
  501: # "$_->{$col} // ''" replaces the equivalent ternary form.
  502: #
  503: # Optimisation: $lval = lc($val) is computed once before the grep.  Without this

  Mutants (Total: 1, Killed: 1, Survived: 0)
  504: # precompute, every case-insensitive operator (eq, ne, contains, starts) would

  Mutants (Total: 1, Killed: 1, Survived: 0)
  505: # call lc($val) O(N) times for N records -- a pure O(N) waste since $val is

  Mutants (Total: 3, Killed: 3, Survived: 0)
  506: # constant within a single filter application.

  Mutants (Total: 3, Killed: 3, Survived: 0)
  507: sub _apply_filter_spec {

  Mutants (Total: 3, Killed: 3, Survived: 0)
  508: 	my ($records, $spec) = @_;

  Mutants (Total: 3, Killed: 3, Survived: 0)
  509: 	my ($col, $op, $val) = split /:/, $spec, 3;
  510: 	return $records unless length($col // '') && defined $op && length $op;
  511: 	$val //= '';
  512: 	my $lval = lc $val;	# precompute once; avoids O(N) redundant lc() inside grep
  513: 	return [grep {
  514: 		my $cell = $_->{$col} // '';
  515: 		$op eq 'eq'       ? lc($cell) eq $lval                :
  516: 		$op eq 'ne'       ? lc($cell) ne $lval                :
  517: 		$op eq 'contains' ? index(lc($cell), $lval) != -1     :
  518: 		$op eq 'starts'   ? index(lc($cell), $lval) == 0      :
  519: 		$op eq 'lt'       ? $cell <  $val                     :
  520: 		$op eq 'le'       ? $cell <= $val                     :
  521: 		$op eq 'gt'       ? $cell >  $val                     :
  522: 		$op eq 'ge'       ? $cell >= $val                     :
  523: 		$op eq 'empty'    ? $cell eq ''                        :
  524: 		$op eq 'notempty' ? $cell ne ''                        :
  525: 		1
  526: 	} @$records];
  527: }

  574: # _filter_specs_to_criteria( \@specs, $source ) -> ( \%criteria, \@residual_specs )
  575: #
  576: # Purpose: Map the f= filter vocabulary onto Database::Abstraction / Database::Join
  577: #          criteria hashrefs so the backend can apply pushable filters before
  578: #          paginating.  Specs that cannot be safely pushed are returned as
  579: #          @residual_specs for subsequent in-memory application.
  580: #
  581: # Pushdown is only performed when $source->dbi_source() confirms a SQL (SQLite)
  582: # backend.  For CSV (slurp path), in-memory, XLSX, BerkeleyDB and URL sources
  583: # everything is placed in @residual_specs so the existing _apply_filter_spec
  584: # Perl path handles them.
  585: #
  586: # Pushable mapping (SQLite only):
  587: #   eq / ne         -- LIKE / NOT LIKE without wildcards.  SQLite LIKE is
  588: #                      case-insensitive for ASCII, matching the existing Perl
  589: #                      lc() semantics.  Values containing % or _ are pushed to
  590: #                      residual to avoid LIKE metacharacter conflicts.
  591: #   contains        -- LIKE '%val%'.  Residual when value contains % or _.
  592: #   starts          -- LIKE 'val%'.   Residual when value contains % or _.
  593: #   lt / le / gt / ge -- SQL numeric comparison.  Residual when the value is
  594: #                      not a plain number (avoids column-affinity surprises
  595: #                      with currency or comma-formatted values).
  596: #   empty / notempty -- Always residual.  Must match both SQL NULL and the
  597: #                      literal empty string; cannot be expressed as a single
  598: #                      D::A criterion without an -or grouping.
  599: #
  600: # Column collision: if a column already has a pushable criterion, the second
  601: # spec for that column is placed in @residual_specs.  This keeps the AND logic
  602: # simple and safe; callers with multiple conditions on one column (e.g. a range)
  603: # still get correct results via the in-memory fallback.

  924: # _fetch_page( $self, $source, \%params ) -> ( $records, $has_next )
  925: #   params: per_page  -- page size (required)
  926: #           offset    -- 0-based row offset (required)
  927: #           dedup     -- truthy when ?d=1 dedup is requested
  928: #           sort_col  -- column name to sort by (optional)
  929: #           sort_dir  -- 'asc' or 'desc' (default 'asc'; ignored unless sort_col set)
  930: #
  931: # Purpose: Fetch and reduce one page of records from $source, honouring the
  932: #          reduce-before-paginate ordering contract.  Two modes:
  933: #
  934: #   Fast path (no f= filters, no dedup): uses the N+1 trick
  935: #     selectall_arrayref(limit => per_page+1, offset => offset).  has_next is
  936: #     determined by the extra row before it is popped.
  937: #
  938: #   Reduction path (f= filters or dedup present): materialises the full result
  939: #     in memory, applies all f= filters with _apply_filter_spec (case-insensitive),
  940: #     applies dedup if requested, then paginates in Perl.  This guarantees that
  941: #     the Nth page contains the Nth page of the *reduced* set, not the Nth page
  942: #     of the raw set reduced to fewer rows.  Memory cost: the full reduced set.
  943: #
  944: # Sort: sort_col/sort_dir are forwarded to selectall_arrayref as
  945: #   sort_by => [$sort_col, $sort_dir].  Both Database::Abstraction 0.49+
  946: #   and Database::Join 0.009.0 honour this parameter natively.

  948: # Returns (undef, 0) when the underlying fetch throws; the caller must handle
  949: # $records being undef.

 1464: # _inside_dir($path, $dir) -> $bool
 1465: #
 1466: # True when $path names $dir itself or something below it.  abs2rel asks the
 1467: # file system what the relationship between the two is, so a textual prefix
 1468: # cannot make ".../data-archive" look like it is inside ".../data", and the
 1469: # comparison is made with the platform's separator rather than a hard-coded
 1470: # slash.
 1471: sub _inside_dir {
 1472: 	my ($path, $dir) = @_;
 1473: 	return 0 unless length $path && length $dir;
 1474: 	return 1 if $path eq $dir;
 1475: 
 1476: 	my $rel = eval { File::Spec->abs2rel($path, $dir) };
 1477: 	return 0 unless defined $rel && length $rel;
 1478: 	return 0 if File::Spec->file_name_is_absolute($rel);
 1479: 	return $rel eq '..' || $rel =~ m{\A\.\.[\\/]} ? 0 : 1;
 1480: }

 2773: # _decode_cell($s) -> $character_string
 2774: #
 2775: # Purpose: Ensure $s is a Perl character string, not raw UTF-8 bytes.
 2776: #          URL-fetched data (via LWP::UserAgent::Cached + D::A HTML parsing)
 2777: #          arrives as byte strings without the UTF-8 flag set.  encode_json
 2778: #          then treats each byte as a Latin-1 code point and re-encodes it,
 2779: #          producing mojibake (e.g. an o-umlaut character displayed as two garbage bytes).  This helper
 2780: #          upgrades the flag in-place on a copy so the original hash key is
 2781: #          unchanged but the value passed to HTML::D3 is a proper character.
 2782: # Entry:   $s -- any defined scalar (undef returned as-is).
 2783: # Exit:    The same string value, but with the UTF-8 flag set if it was absent.
 2784: sub _decode_cell {
 2785: 	my $s = shift;
 2786: 	return $s unless defined $s;
 2787: 	utf8::decode($s) unless utf8::is_utf8($s);
 2788: 	$s;
 2789: }

 2791: # _serialize_csv($records, \@columns) -> $utf8_string
 2792: #
 2793: # Purpose: Build a complete RFC 4180 CSV document (header + data rows) from
 2794: #          $records.  Shared by _render_csv (HTTP download) and the csv branch
 2795: #          of export_write (filesystem write) to eliminate duplicated logic.
 2796: # Entry:   $records is an arrayref of hashrefs; $columns is an arrayref of names.
 2797: # Exit:    Returns a UTF-8 Perl string ending with CRLF.
 2798: #
 2799: # Optimisation: rows are collected in @lines and joined in one operation.
 2800: # The previous pattern ($out .= _csv_row(...) per row) triggers O(N) string
 2801: # reallocations; joining a pre-built array is a single O(total_bytes) allocation.
 2802: # For exports of thousands of rows the difference is measurable.
 2803: #
 2804: # XS note: for very large exports, replacing _csv_row with Text::CSV_XS
 2805: # (available from CPAN) would further reduce serialisation time -- its C-level
 2806: # implementation is roughly 5-10x faster than the pure-Perl version here.
 2807: sub _serialize_csv {
●2808 → 2810 → 2813 2808: 	my ($records, $columns) = @_;
 2809: 	my @lines = _csv_row(@$columns);
 2810: 	for my $row (@$records) {
 2811: 		push @lines, _csv_row(map { $row->{$_} } @$columns);
 2812: 	}
 2813: 	return encode('UTF-8', join('', @lines));
 2814: }
 2815: 
 2816: # _render_csv($self, $records, \@columns, $name) -> void (renders response)
 2817: #
 2818: # Purpose: Serialise $records to RFC 4180 CSV and emit as a UTF-8 download.
 2819: # Entry:   $name is a safe filename stem (alphanumeric/underscore).
 2820: # Exit:    Renders the Mojolicious response; does not return a meaningful value.
 2821: # Side Effects: Writes HTTP response headers and body.
 2822: sub _render_csv :Protected ($self, $records, $columns, $name) {
 2823: 	$self->res->headers->content_type('text/csv; charset=UTF-8');
 2824: 	$self->res->headers->content_disposition(qq{attachment; filename="${name}.csv"});
 2825: 	$self->render(data => _serialize_csv($records, $columns));
 2826: }
 2827: 
 2828: # _render_sqlite($self, $records, \@columns, $name) -> void (renders response)
 2829: #
 2830: # Purpose: Write $records to a SQLite database and emit it as a binary download.
 2831: # Entry:   $name is a safe filename stem.
 2832: # Exit:    Renders the Mojolicious response.
 2833: # Side Effects: Creates and unlinks a temporary file; writes HTTP response.
 2834: sub _render_sqlite :Protected ($self, $records, $columns, $name) {
 2835: 	my $data = $self->_write_sqlite_db($records, $columns);
 2836: 	$self->res->headers->content_type('application/vnd.sqlite3');
 2837: 	$self->res->headers->content_disposition(qq{attachment; filename="${name}.db"});
 2838: 	$self->render(data => $data);
 2839: }
 2840: 
 2841: # _render_json($self, $records, \@columns, $name) -> void (renders response)
 2842: #
 2843: # Purpose: Emit $records as a JSON array download.
 2844: # Entry:   $records is an arrayref of hashrefs; $name is a safe filename stem.
 2845: # Exit:    Renders the Mojolicious response.
 2846: # Side Effects: Writes HTTP response headers and body.
 2847: sub _render_json :Protected ($self, $records, $columns, $name) {
 2848: 	$self->res->headers->content_disposition(qq{attachment; filename="${name}.json"});
 2849: 	$self->render(json => $records);
 2850: }

 2919: =head4 DOMAIN CONSTRAINTS: :table
 2920: 
 2921: C<:table> is validated against C<\A[A-Za-z_][A-Za-z0-9_]*\z> before C<open_table>
 2922: is ever called.  The first character must be a letter (A-Z, a-z) or an
 2923: underscore; subsequent characters may also include digits (0-9).
 2924: 
 2925: =over 4
 2926: 
 2927: =item Valid partition
 2928: 
 2929: C<sales> (letters only), C<_temp> (underscore-start), C<report_2024>
 2930: (mixed).  A name that is valid but has no backing file returns 200 with
 2931: an error message -- NOT 404.
 2932: 
 2933: =item Invalid partition
 2934: 
 2935: C<1sales> (digit-start, 404), C<my.data> (dot, 404), C<my-data>
 2936: (hyphen, 404), non-ASCII characters (404).
 2937: 
 2938: =item Boundary values
 2939: 
 2940: C<a> (single letter, valid), C<_> (single underscore, valid), C<1>
 2941: (single digit, 404), C<a1> (letter then digit, valid), C<1a> (digit
 2942: then letter, 404).
 2943: 
 2944: =back
 2945: 
 2946: =head4 OUTPUT
 2947: 
 2948: On success renders C<dashboard.html.tt> with table data.
 2949: On error re-renders C<home.html.tt> with an C<error> stash variable.
 2950:

 2952:

 2966:   GET /view/sales      -> 200 HTML table of all sales rows

 3098: 	my @dirs = map {

 3103: 		+{ name => $_->{name}, href => '/open?path=' . url_escape($_->{path}) }
 3104: 	} @{ $listing->{files} };

 3106: 	# Build breadcrumb trail from filesystem root down to $dir.
 3107: 	my @crumbs;
 3108: 	{
 3109: 		my $f = $dir;
 3110: 		while (1) {
 3111: 			my $name = $f->basename;
 3112: 			$name    = '/' unless length $name;
 3113: 			unshift @crumbs, { name => $name, href => '/browse?path=' . url_escape($f->to_string) };
 3114: 			my $parent = $f->dirname;
 3115: 			last if $parent->to_string eq $f->to_string;
 3116: 			$f = $parent;
 3117: 		}
 3118: 	}
 3119: 
 3120: 	my $parent      = $dir->dirname;
 3121: 	my $parent_href = $parent->to_string ne $dir->to_string
 3122: 		? '/browse?path=' . url_escape($parent->to_string)
 3123: 		: undef;

 3138: =head2 open_file
 3139: 
 3140: C<GET /open> -- Open a supported data file from any absolute filesystem path.
 3141: 
 3142: =head3 API SPECIFICATION
 3143: 
 3144: =head4 INPUT
 3145: 

  Mutants (Total: 1, Killed: 1, Survived: 0)
 3146:   ?path=   string   Absolute path to the data file.  Returns 404 when missing,

  Mutants (Total: 2, Killed: 2, Survived: 0)
 3147:                     not a regular file, or the extension is not in SUPPORTED_EXT.
 3148:   ?f=      string   (repeatable) Filter spec: "col:op:val".
 3149: 
 3150: =head4 DOMAIN CONSTRAINTS: ?path=
 3151: 
 3152: =over 4
 3153: 
 3154: =item Extension filter (C<EXT_RE>)
 3155: 
 3156: The basename must match C<\.(?:csv|db|sql|xml|tsv|psv)\z> (case-insensitive).
 3157: The C<\z> anchor (absolute end-of-string) means a URL-encoded trailing
 3158: newline (e.g. C<file.csv%0A> decoded to C<file.csv\n>) does NOT pass --
 3159: the C<\n> falls after the C<\z> boundary and the extension check fails.
 3160: 
 3161: =item Valid partition
 3162: 

  Mutants (Total: 2, Killed: 2, Survived: 0)
 3163: C</data/sales.csv> (lowercase extension), C</tmp/REPORT.CSV> (uppercase
 3164: extension, /i matches), any C<.db>, C<.sql>, C<.xml>, C<.tsv>, C<.psv> regular
 3165: file.
 3166: 
 3167: =item Invalid partition
 3168: 
 3169: Absent C<?path=> (404), non-existent file (404), directory instead of
 3170: file (404), unsupported extension such as C<.txt> (404), empty string
 3171: (404), path containing a C<%0A> (newline) suffix (404).
 3172: 
 3173: =item Double-extension filenames
 3174: 
 3175: A file like C<file.php.csv> passes C<EXT_RE> (last segment is C<.csv>)
 3176: but produces table stem C<file.php> which fails C<DataSource>'s
 3177: C<TABLE_NAME_RE>.  The C<open_table> call is inside C<eval>, so the
 3178: croak is caught and the action returns 200 with a friendly error, not
 3179: a 500.
 3180: 
 3181: =back
 3182: 
 3183: =head4 OUTPUT
 3184: 
 3185: On success renders C<dashboard.html.tt> with C<back_url> pointing to the
 3186: directory's C</browse> page, and C<file_path> set to the resolved absolute
 3187: path (used by the template to record the file in C<localStorage>).
 3188: On error re-renders C<home.html.tt> with C<error>, C<back_url>, C<back_label>.
 3189: 
 3190: =head3 MESSAGES
 3191: 
 3192:   error_file_open   -- DataSource threw during initialisation or fetch_all.
 3193: 

  Mutants (Total: 1, Killed: 1, Survived: 0)
 3194: =head3 FORMAL SPECIFICATION
 3195: 
 3196:   open_file == lambda self .

  Mutants (Total: 2, Killed: 2, Survived: 0)
 3197:     let file = realpath(param('path')) in
 3198:     pre  is_file(file) /\ basename(file) =~ EXT_RE
 3199:     let src = open_table(stem(file), dir=dirname(file)) in
 3200:     post render(dashboard, file_path: file.to_string)
 3201: 
 3202: =head3 EXAMPLE
 3203: 
 3204:   GET /open?path=/data/archive/sales.csv   -> 200, table view
 3205:   GET /open                                -> 404

  Mutants (Total: 1, Killed: 1, Survived: 0)

 3438: =head2 import_url
 3439: 
 3440: C<GET /import> -- Fetch a remote HTML page, extract the first (or selected)
 3441: table, and render it as a sortable data grid.
 3442: 
 3443: =head3 API SPECIFICATION
 3444: 
 3445: =head4 INPUT
 3446: 
 3447:   ?url=          string   Required.  Full http:// or https:// URL of the page
 3448:                           that contains the HTML table.  Returns home with an
 3449:                           error message if the scheme is wrong, the fetch fails,
 3450:                           or no table is found at the specified index.
 3451:   ?table_index=  integer  Zero-based index of the C<< <table> >> to use when
 3452:                           the page contains more than one table (default: 0).
 3453:   ?f=            string   (repeatable) Filter spec: "col:op:val".
 3454: 
 3455: =head4 OUTPUT
 3456: 
 3457: On success renders C<dashboard.html.tt> with the same stash shape as C<open_file>,
 3458: plus C<source_url> (the original URL) for C<localStorage> tracking.
 3459: 
 3460: On error re-renders C<home.html.tt> with an C<error> stash variable.
 3461: 
 3462: =head3 MESSAGES
 3463: 
 3464:   error_url_required  -- empty or missing C<url> param
 3465:   error_url_invalid   -- URL does not begin with http:// or https://
 3466:   error_url_fetch     -- LWP fetch failure or no table found at the index
 3467: 
 3468: =head3 EXAMPLE
 3469: 
 3470:   GET /import?url=https://matrix.perl-magpie.org/dist/Database-Abstraction/
 3471:   GET /import?url=https://example.com/data.html&table_index=2
 3472: 
 3473: =cut

 3540: =head2 columns_api
 3541: 
 3542: C<GET /api/columns> -- Return column names for a table or file as JSON.
 3543: 
 3544: Used by the join UI to populate the right-key dropdown without a page reload.
 3545: 
 3546: =head3 API SPECIFICATION
 3547: 
 3548: =head4 INPUT
 3549: 
 3550:   ?table=  string   Table name from C<data_dir>.
 3551:   ?path=   string   Absolute path to a data file.
 3552:   ?spec=   string   Unified spec: C<"table:name"> or C<"path:/abs/path">.
 3553:                     Parsed only when C<table> and C<path> are both absent.
 3554: 
 3555: One of C<table>, C<path>, or C<spec> must be present; C<table> takes
 3556: precedence over C<path>, and both take precedence over C<spec>.
 3557: 
 3558: =head4 OUTPUT
 3559: 
 3560:   200 application/json   { "columns": ["col1", "col2", ...] }
 3561:   404 application/json   { "error": "not found" }
 3562: 
 3563: =head3 MESSAGES
 3564: 
 3565: None produced by this action directly; internal errors yield a 404.
 3566: 
 3567: =head3 FORMAL SPECIFICATION
 3568: 
 3569:   columns_api == lambda self .
 3570:     let src = open_spec(table_param or path_param or parse_spec(spec_param)) in
 3571:     pre  src /= undef
 3572:     post render_json({ columns: get_columns(src) })
 3573: 
 3574: =head3 EXAMPLE
 3575: 
 3576:   GET /api/columns?table=sales            -> {"columns":["product","region","amount"]}
 3577:   GET /api/columns?spec=table:sales       -> {"columns":["product","region","amount"]}
 3578:   GET /api/columns?spec=path:/data/f.csv  -> {"columns":[...]}
 3579:   GET /api/columns?table=noexist          -> 404
 3580: 
 3581: =cut
 3582:

 3798: =head2 combine_tables
 3799: 
 3800: C<GET /combine> -- Stack rows from two or more tables vertically into a single
 3801: unified view.
 3802: 
 3803: Unlike C</join> (which appends columns from a matching row in a second table),
 3804: C</combine> appends the I<rows> from a second table beneath the rows of the
 3805: first.  All columns from all sources appear as headers; where a row has no
 3806: value for a particular column (because that column did not exist in its source
 3807: file) the cell is left blank.
 3808: 
 3809: This is equivalent to a SQL C<UNION ALL> across heterogeneous schemas.
 3810: 

  Mutants (Total: 1, Killed: 1, Survived: 0)
 3811: =head3 API SPECIFICATION
 3812: 
 3813: =head4 INPUT
 3814: 
 3815:   ?l=    string   (required) Left table spec: "table:name" or "path:/abs/path".
 3816:                   Returns 404 if unresolvable.
 3817:   ?c=    string   (repeatable) Additional table spec to stack beneath the
 3818:                   current result.  Invalid or non-existent specs are silently
 3819:                   skipped.
 3820:   ?f=    string   (repeatable) Result filter: "col:op:val".
 3821: 

  Mutants (Total: 3, Killed: 3, Survived: 0)
 3822: =head4 OUTPUT
 3823: 
 3824: Renders C<dashboard.html.tt> with the combined result set.
 3825: 
 3826: =head3 MESSAGES
 3827: 
 3828:   error_table_open   -- Left table fetch threw (re-renders home with error).
 3829: 
 3830: =head3 FORMAL SPECIFICATION
 3831: 
 3832:   combine_tables == lambda self .
 3833:     let left = open_spec(param('l')) in
 3834:     pre  left /= undef
 3835:     let sources  = [left] ++ map(open_spec, param_list('c')) in
 3836:     let combined = _combine_tables(sources) in
 3837:     let filtered = fold(_apply_filter_spec, combined, param_list('f')) in
 3838:     post render(dashboard, records: filtered)
 3839: 
 3840: =head3 EXAMPLE
 3841: 
 3842:   GET /combine?l=table:cats&c=table:dogs
 3843:     -> unified view: all cat and dog rows; Species/Name/Color/Breed/Eye Color
 3844:        appear for both; Environment (cats-only) and Sex/Fixed (dogs-only)
 3845:        are blank where the source file lacks that column.

  Mutants (Total: 2, Killed: 2, Survived: 0)
 4340: C<eval {}> + C<if ($@)>).  Mojolicious dispatch and DESTROY-phase evals can
 4341: leave C<$@> set before the action runs; checking C<if ($@)> after a new eval
 4342: may fire falsely on that stale value.  C<eval { move_to(); 1 }> returns 1 on

 6624: 	$bins = int($bins);
 6625: 	$bins = 1   if $bins < 1;

 6627: 
 6628: 	# Server-side binning: compute equal-width bins over the data range.
 6629: 	# Values equal to $max land in the highest bin (no overflow bar).
 6630: 	# Uses the same bin-boundary logic as Calc.pm bin() to stay consistent with
 6631: 	# ?c=band:bin(col,width) computed columns on the same data.
 6632: 	use POSIX qw(floor);
 6633: 	my $min_v = min(@values);
 6634: 	my $max_v = max(@values);
 6635: 	my @bin_data;
 6636: 	if ($min_v == $max_v) {
 6637: 		# All values identical: one bin.
 6638: 		push @bin_data, { label => "$min_v", count => scalar @values, lo => $min_v, hi => $min_v };
 6639: 	} else {
 6640: 		my $raw_w = ($max_v - $min_v) / $bins;
 6641: 		# Round up to a "nice" width using 10 significant digits (same guard as Calc bin()).
 6642: 		my $width = sprintf('%.10g', $raw_w) + 0;
 6643: 		$width = $raw_w if $width <= 0;  # safety: never produce zero width
 6644: 		my $fmt = ($width == int($width)) ? '%d' : '%g';
 6645: 
 6646: 		# Build bin boundaries.
 6647: 		my @bin_counts;
 6648: 		my @bin_los;
 6649: 		for my $i (0 .. $bins - 1) {
 6650: 			push @bin_los, $min_v + $i * $width;
 6651: 			push @bin_counts, 0;
 6652: 		}
 6653: 
 6654: 		# Assign each value to a bin.
 6655: 		for my $v (@values) {
 6656: 			my $idx = floor(sprintf('%.10g', ($v - $min_v) / $width) + 0);
 6657: 			$idx = $bins - 1 if $idx >= $bins;  # max value -> last bin
 6658: 			$idx = 0         if $idx < 0;
 6659: 			$bin_counts[$idx]++;
 6660: 		}
 6661: 
 6662: 		for my $i (0 .. $bins - 1) {
 6663: 			next unless $bin_counts[$i];
 6664: 			my $lo  = $bin_los[$i];
 6665: 			my $hi  = $lo + $width;
 6666: 			my $lbl = sprintf("[${fmt},${fmt})", $lo, $hi);
 6667: 			push @bin_data, { label => $lbl, count => $bin_counts[$i], lo => $lo, hi => $hi };
 6668: 		}
 6669: 	}
 6670: 
 6671: 	my ($source_url, $source_accessed) = $self->_url_attribution;
 6672: 
 6673: 	require HTML::D3;
 6674: 	my $title   = "Distribution of $col";

 6676: 		->render_histogram_snippet(\@bin_data, {
 6677: 			binned  => 1,
 6678: 			x_label => $col,
 6679: 		});

 6682: 	utf8::decode($chart_html) unless utf8::is_utf8($chart_html);
 6683: 
 6684: 	my %chart_spec = (
 6685: 		kind    => 'histogram',
 6686: 		title   => $title,
 6687: 		encode  => { value => $col },
 6688: 		options => { bins => $bins },
 6689: 	);
 6690: 	my ($platform, $language) = $self->_resolve_template;
 6691: 	$self->render(
 6692: 		handler          => 'tt',
 6693: 		template         => "$platform/$language/histogram",
 6694: 		format           => 'html',
 6695: 		title            => $title,
 6696: 		chart_html       => $chart_html,
 6697: 		chart            => \%chart_spec,
 6698: 		value_count      => scalar @values,
 6699: 		bins             => $bins,
 6700: 		back_url         => ($back2 // $back),
 6701: 		back_label       => ($back2 ? ($back2_lbl || 'Back') : 'Back to table'),
 6702: 		($back2 ? (back2_url => $back, back2_label => 'Back to table') : ()),
 6703: 		source_url       => $source_url,
 6704: 		source_accessed  => $source_accessed,
 6705: 	);
 6706: }

 6708: # ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
 6709: # Map  (GET /map)
 6710: #
 6711: # Phase 6.1/6.2: one route, one template, two ways for a row to get a
 6712: # position.  With lat= and lon= the columns already hold coordinates; with
 6713: # loc=<column> every distinct value of that column is geocoded once, by
 6714: # Database::BI::Model::Geocode, and the resolved places are remembered in the
 6715: # model store so a second visit asks nothing.  Everything before that - the

 6950: 
 6951: # _map_popup($heading, $rows, \@extras) -> $html
 6952: #
 6953: # The popup for one marker: what it is, how many rows it stands for, and the
 6954: # columns the request asked for.  Leaflet treats a popup string as HTML, and a
 6955: # place name out of a user's file is not markup, so every value is escaped here.
 6956: sub _map_popup {
 6957: 	my ($heading, $rows, $extras) = @_;
 6958: 
 6959: 	my @lines = '<strong>' . xml_escape($heading // '') . '</strong>';
 6960: 	push @lines, $rows . ($rows == 1 ? ' row' : ' rows');
 6961: 	push @lines, map { xml_escape($_->[0]) . ': ' . xml_escape($_->[1]) }
 6962: 		grep { defined($_->[1]) && length($_->[1]) } @{ $extras // [] };
 6963: 
 6964: 	return join('<br>', @lines);
 6965: }
 6966: 
 6967: # _map_limit($value, $default) -> $integer
 6968: #
 6969: # A positive integer from a config value or request parameter, or the default.
 6970: # An absent, empty or nonsensical map setting is an operator's typo, not a data
 6971: # error, so the page falls back instead of refusing.
 6972: sub _map_limit {
 6973: 	my ($value, $default) = @_;
 6974: 
 6975: 	return $default unless defined($value) && ($value =~ /\A\d+\z/) && ($value > 0);
 6976: 
 6977: 	return $value + 0;
 6978: }
 6979: 
 6980: # _iso8601_utc() -> $string
 6981: #
 6982: # When a geocode cache was built, in the form the model store records.
 6983: sub _iso8601_utc {
 6984: 	require POSIX;
 6985: 
 6986: 	return POSIX::strftime('%Y-%m-%dT%H:%M:%SZ', gmtime());
 6987: }
 6988: 
 6989: 1;

 6991: __END__
 6992: 
 6993: =head1 NAME
 6994: 
 6995: Database::BI::Controller::Dashboard - Home picker, filesystem browser, table
 6996: viewer, left-join engine, result filter, export, and file upload
 6997: 
 6998: =head1 SYNOPSIS
 6999: 
 7000: All routes in C<Database::BI> are handled by this controller.  You do not
 7001: call its methods directly -- Mojolicious dispatches HTTP requests to them
 7002: automatically.  The examples below show browser URLs and their curl
 7003: equivalents.
 7004: 
 7005: B<Open the home page and see all tables in the data directory:>
 7006: 
 7007:   # Browser
 7008:   http://localhost:3000/
 7009: 
 7010:   # curl
 7011:   curl http://localhost:3000/
 7012: 
 7013: B<View a single table (file: data/sales.csv):>
 7014: 
 7015:   # Browser
 7016:   http://localhost:3000/view/sales
 7017: 
 7018:   # curl
 7019:   curl http://localhost:3000/view/sales
 7020: 
 7021: B<Filter rows -- show only rows where region equals "North":>
 7022: 
 7023:   # Browser (add ?f=col:op:val to any view URL)
 7024:   http://localhost:3000/view/sales?f=region:eq:North
 7025: 
 7026:   # Multiple filters -- "North" AND amount greater than 100:
 7027:   http://localhost:3000/view/sales?f=region:eq:North&f=amount:gt:100
 7028: 
 7029:   # curl
 7030:   curl 'http://localhost:3000/view/sales?f=region:eq:North'
 7031: 
 7032: B<Join two tables -- left-join sales with products on the product column:>
 7033: 
 7034:   # Browser
 7035:   http://localhost:3000/join?l=table:sales&j=table:products|product|name
 7036: 
 7037:   # The j= parameter is: right-table-spec | left-key | right-key
 7038:   # You can chain multiple joins:
 7039:   http://localhost:3000/join?l=table:sales&j=table:products|product|name&j=table:regions|region|id
 7040: 
 7041: B<Open a file anywhere on the filesystem (not just in data/):>
 7042: 
 7043:   http://localhost:3000/open?path=/home/user/reports/q3.csv
 7044: 
 7045: B<Download the current view as a CSV file:>
 7046: 
 7047:   # Uses the same l=, j=, f= parameters as /join
 7048:   http://localhost:3000/export?l=table:sales&format=csv
 7049: 
 7050:   # Download as a SQLite database file instead:
 7051:   http://localhost:3000/export?l=table:sales&format=sqlite
 7052: 
 7053:   # Download a filtered + joined result:
 7054:   http://localhost:3000/export?l=table:sales&j=table:products|product|name&f=region:eq:North&format=csv
 7055: 
 7056: B<Save the current view to a file on the server (instead of downloading):>
 7057: 
 7058:   # POST with form fields; format is inferred from the filename extension
 7059:   curl -X POST http://localhost:3000/export \
 7060:        -F l=table:sales \
 7061:        -F dir=/home/user/exports \
 7062:        -F filename=report.csv
 7063: 
 7064:   # Save as SQLite:
 7065:   curl -X POST http://localhost:3000/export \
 7066:        -F l=table:sales \
 7067:        -F dir=/home/user/exports \
 7068:        -F filename=report.sql
 7069: 
 7070: B<Get the column list for a table (used by the join UI):>
 7071: 
 7072:   curl http://localhost:3000/api/columns?table=sales
 7073:   # Returns: {"columns":["product","region","amount","date"]}
 7074: 
 7075: B<Check when a file was last modified (used by the tooltip on the home page):>
 7076: 
 7077:   curl 'http://localhost:3000/api/stat?path=/data/sales.csv'
 7078:   # Returns: {"exists":true,"path":"/data/sales.csv","mtime":1700000000,"size":1234}
 7079: 
 7080: B<Browse the filesystem to find a data file:>
 7081: 
 7082:   http://localhost:3000/browse
 7083:   http://localhost:3000/browse?path=/home/user/data
 7084: 
 7085: B<Upload a data file by dropping it onto the page (multipart form POST):>
 7086: 
 7087:   curl -X POST http://localhost:3000/upload \
 7088:        -F file=@/home/user/data/sales.csv
 7089:   # Returns: {"url":"/open?path=/.../.uploads/.../sales.csv","path":"/.../.uploads/.../sales.csv"}
 7090: 
 7091: =head2 graph_view
 7092: 
 7093: C<GET /graph> -- Render a D3.js v7 line chart from the current data pipeline.
 7094: 
 7095: Accepts the same pipeline parameters as L</join_tables> (C<l=>, C<j=>,
 7096: C<c=>, C<f=>, C<d=>) plus:
 7097: 
 7098: =over 4
 7099: 
 7100: =item C<x=E<lt>colE<gt>>
 7101: 
 7102: Column name to use as the X axis (required).  Any data type is accepted;
 7103: values are displayed as categorical labels.
 7104: 
 7105: =item C<y=E<lt>colE<gt>>
 7106: 
 7107: Column name to use as the Y axis (required).  Non-numeric characters
 7108: (currency symbols, commas, etc.) are stripped before comparison; rows
 7109: where the value is still non-numeric after stripping are silently skipped.
 7110: 
 7111: =item C<back=E<lt>urlE<gt>>
 7112: 
 7113: URL for the "Back to table" link rendered in the graph toolbar.  Defaults
 7114: to C</> when absent.  The dashboard JS sets this to the current page URL
 7115: before navigating.
 7116: 
 7117: =back
 7118: 
 7119: =head3 API SPECIFICATION
 7120: 
 7121: =head4 INPUT
 7122: 
 7123:   l=<spec>     string   Left-table spec (required).  Same syntax as /join.
 7124:   x=<col>      string   X-axis column name (required).
 7125:   y=<col>      string   Y-axis column name (required; must be numeric).
 7126:   back=<url>   string   Back-link URL (optional; default "/").
 7127:   j=, c=, f=, d=        Pipeline params -- see L</join_tables>.
 7128: 
 7129: =head4 OUTPUT
 7130: 
 7131:   200 text/html   TT-rendered graph page.  The HTML::D3 snippet embeds a
 7132:                   C<const data = [...]> JSON array where each element has:
 7133:                     { label: <x-value>, value: <y-value>,
 7134:                       extra: { <col>: <val>, ... } }
 7135:                   C<extra> contains every column not used as X or Y, so
 7136:                   the hover tooltip shows the complete row for each point.
 7137:                   The chart supports brush-to-zoom; a "Reset zoom" button
 7138:                   appears after a brush selection.  The toolbar shows the
 7139:                   count of plotted data points.
 7140: 
 7141:   400 text/plain  C<x=> or C<y=> absent, or named column not found.
 7142:   404 text/plain  Data source could not be opened.
 7143: 
 7144: =head3 MESSAGES
 7145: 
 7146:   Missing x or y column parameter          C<x=> or C<y=> query param absent.
 7147:   Column not found: <name>                  Named column absent from result set.
 7148:   Could not open data source                Left-table spec unresolvable or 404.
 7149:   No plottable data: no rows have valid ... All rows lack a numeric Y value.
 7150: 
 7151: =head3 EXAMPLE
 7152: 
 7153:   GET /graph?l=table:sales&x=product&y=amount
 7154: 
 7155: Renders a line chart of C<amount> (Y) against C<product> (X).  Mousing over
 7156: a dot shows a tooltip with the product name, the amount, and every other
 7157: column in that row (e.g. C<region>, C<date>).
 7158: 
 7159: =head3 FORMAL SPECIFICATION
 7160: 
 7161:   graph_view : Controller Ã— Params â†’ HTML | Error
 7162: 
 7163:   let pipeline = run_export_pipeline(l, j, c, f, d)
 7164:   let pairs    = [ [row[x], num(row[y]), row \ {x,y}]
 7165:                    | row âˆˆ pipeline.records,  num(row[y]) â‰  âŠ¥ ]
 7166: 
 7167:   pre  x âˆˆ params âˆ§ y âˆˆ params           else 400
 7168:   pre  x âˆˆ pipeline.cols                  else 400
 7169:   pre  y âˆˆ pipeline.cols                  else 400
 7170:   pre  pairs â‰  []                          else 200 "No plottable data"
 7171:   post HTML::D3->render_line_chart_snippet(pairs) embedded in TT layout
 7172: 
 7173: =cut
 7174: 
 7175: =head2 pie_view
 7176: 
 7177: C<GET /pie> -- Render a D3.js v7 animated pie chart from the current data
 7178: pipeline.
 7179: 
 7180: Each row's category value (C<cat=>) is used as a slice label; the numeric
 7181: value column (C<val=>) is summed per category.  Accounting-notation
 7182: negatives such as C<(1,234.56)> are handled automatically.  Slices are
 7183: sorted by total descending; when there are more than 12 distinct categories
 7184: the smallest ones are collapsed into a single "Other" slice.
 7185: 
 7186: Clicking any slice or legend entry navigates back to the table view with
 7187: an C<f=cat:eq:value> filter applied automatically, so the user can drill
 7188: into the data behind a slice without leaving the application.
 7189: 
 7190: =head3 API SPECIFICATION
 7191: 
 7192: =head4 INPUT
 7193: 
 7194:   l=<spec>    string   Left-table spec (required).  Same syntax as /join.
 7195:   cat=<col>   string   Category column name (required; used as slice labels).
 7196:   val=<col>   string   Numeric value column name (required; values are summed).
 7197:   donut=1     flag     Render as a donut chart with a hole in the centre (optional).
 7198:   back=<url>  string   URL for the "Back to table" breadcrumb link (optional; default "/").
 7199:   f=<spec>    string   Result filter (repeatable; same syntax as /join).
 7200: 
 7201: =head4 OUTPUT
 7202: 
 7203:   200 text/html   TT-rendered pie chart page containing:
 7204:                   - Two-level breadcrumb (Home > Back to table).
 7205:                   - Export buttons for SVG and PNG (legend is composited in).
 7206:                   - An animated D3.js pie or donut chart with a colour-coded
 7207:                     legend.  Each slice and legend entry is clickable and
 7208:                     navigates to the filtered table view.
 7209:                   - Slice count (e.g. "7 slices").
 7210: 
 7211:   200 text/plain  "No plottable data" when every row has a non-numeric or
 7212:                   missing value column (HTTP 200, not 4xx, so the page can
 7213:                   render a friendly message).
 7214: 
 7215:   400 text/plain  C<cat=> or C<val=> absent, or named column not found.
 7216:   404 text/plain  Data source could not be opened.
 7217: 
 7218: =head4 DOMAIN CONSTRAINTS
 7219: 
 7220:   cat=    Any column name that exists in the result set.  Cell values are
 7221:           used verbatim as slice labels.  Empty-string cells are skipped.
 7222: 
 7223:   val=    Must be a column whose cells contain numbers (after stripping
 7224:           currency symbols, commas, and handling accounting-notation negatives).
 7225:           Rows where the value is still non-numeric after stripping are skipped.
 7226:           Negative totals are included in the chart (slice values may be negative
 7227:           if the source data contains credits or reversals).
 7228: 
 7229:   donut=  Any truthy value (e.g. "1") enables the donut hole.  Absent or
 7230:           "0" renders a solid pie.
 7231: 
 7232: =head3 MESSAGES
 7233: 
 7234:   Missing cat or val column parameter    C<cat=> or C<val=> query param absent.
 7235:   Column not found: <name>               Named column absent from the result set.
 7236:   Could not open data source             Left-table spec unresolvable or 404.
 7237:   No plottable data: ...                 All rows lack a valid numeric value.
 7238: 
 7239: =head3 EXAMPLE
 7240: 
 7241:   GET /pie?l=table:sales&cat=region&val=amount&back=/view/sales
 7242: 
 7243: Renders a pie chart of total C<amount> per C<region>.  Clicking the
 7244: "North" slice navigates to C</view/sales?f=region:eq:North>.
 7245: 
 7246: With a donut hole:
 7247: 
 7248:   GET /pie?l=table:sales&cat=region&val=amount&donut=1
 7249: 
 7250: =head3 FORMAL SPECIFICATION
 7251: 
 7252:   pie_view : Controller x Params -> HTML | Error
 7253: 
 7254:   let pipeline = run_export_pipeline(l, j, f, d)
 7255:   let totals   = { cat => sum { val(row) | row in pipeline.records,
 7256:                                            cat(row) != '', num(val(row)) != bot }
 7257:                  | cat in range(cat_col) }
 7258: 
 7259:   pre  cat in params /\ val in params     else 400
 7260:   pre  cat in pipeline.cols               else 400
 7261:   pre  val in pipeline.cols               else 400
 7262:   pre  totals != {}                        else 200 "No plottable data"
 7263:   post HTML::D3->render_pie_chart_snippet(slices) embedded in TT layout
 7264:        where slices = sort_desc { |s.value| | s in totals }
 7265: 
 7266: =cut
 7267: 
 7268: =head2 clear_uploads
 7269: 
 7270: C<POST /uploads/clear> -- Delete every file from the C<.uploads/> staging
 7271: directory and return the total disk space recovered.
 7272: 
 7273: The C<.uploads/> directory accumulates files from drag-and-drop uploads.
 7274: It grows indefinitely; this action lets users reclaim that space without
 7275: needing shell access.
 7276: 
 7277: =head3 API SPECIFICATION
 7278: 
 7279: =head4 INPUT
 7280: 
 7281: No parameters.  The C<.uploads/> path is always the one under C<app->home>.
 7282: 
 7283: =head4 OUTPUT
 7284: 
 7285:   200 application/json   { "freed": <bytes>, "count": <n> }
 7286: 
 7287: C<freed> is the total number of bytes removed.  C<count> is the number of
 7288: files deleted.  Both are 0 when the directory is absent or already empty.
 7289: 
 7290: =cut
 7291: 
 7292: =head2 scatter_view
 7293: 
 7294: C<GET /scatter> -- Render a D3.js scatter plot or bubble chart.
 7295: 
 7296: Accepts the same C<l=>, C<j=>, C<f=> pipeline params as C</join>, plus:
 7297: 
 7298:   x=<col>        X-axis column (required; numeric)
 7299:   y=<col>        Y-axis column (required; numeric)
 7300:   series=<col>   Colour-by category column (optional)
 7301:   size=<col>     Bubble-radius numeric column (optional)
 7302:   scheme=<name>  D3 colour scheme (default tableau10; only used with series=)
 7303:   min_r=N        Minimum bubble radius in px (default 3; range 1-40)
 7304:   max_r=N        Maximum bubble radius in px (default 15; range 1-40)
 7305:   back=<url>     URL for the "Back to table" breadcrumb link
 7306: 
 7307: =head3 API SPECIFICATION
 7308: 
 7309: =head4 INPUT
 7310: 
 7311:   l        spec    Required.  Left table spec (table:name or path:/abs/path).
 7312:   x        string  Required.  Numeric X-axis column name.
 7313:   y        string  Required.  Numeric Y-axis column name.
 7314:   series   string  Optional.  Categorical colour-dimension column.
 7315:   size     string  Optional.  Numeric bubble-radius column.
 7316:   scheme   string  Optional.  One of: tableau10 category10 set2 set3 paired.
 7317:   min_r    int     Optional.  Minimum radius 1-40 (default 3).
 7318:   max_r    int     Optional.  Maximum radius 1-40 (default 15).
 7319:   back     url     Optional.  Safe back-link URL (sanitised by _safe_back_url).
 7320: 
 7321: =head4 OUTPUT
 7322: 
 7323:   200 text/html    Rendered scatter chart page.
 7324:   400 text/plain   Missing x= or y= parameter.
 7325:   400 text/plain   Column not found: <col>.
 7326:   400 text/plain   Unknown colour scheme: <scheme>  (only when series= is set).
 7327:   404 text/plain   "Could not open data source" when l= is absent/invalid.
 7328: 
 7329: =head3 FORMAL SPECIFICATION
 7330: 
 7331:   scatter_view == lambda self .
 7332:     pre  length(x_col) > 0 /\ length(y_col) > 0
 7333:          /\ x_col in columns /\ y_col in columns
 7334:          /\ (series_col != '' => series_col in columns)
 7335:          /\ (size_col   != '' => size_col   in columns)
 7336:          /\ (series_col != '' => scheme in SCATTER_SCHEMES)
 7337:          /\ 1 <= min_r <= 40 /\ 1 <= max_r <= 40
 7338:     post render(scatter, pairs)
 7339: 
 7340: =head3 EXAMPLE
 7341: 
 7342:   GET /scatter?l=table:sales&x=quantity&y=amount&series=region
 7343:     -> 200 HTML page containing D3 scatter chart SVG
 7344: 
 7345: =cut
 7346: 
 7347: =head2 stacked_view
 7348: 
 7349: C<GET /stacked> -- Render a D3.js stacked bar chart.
 7350: 
 7351: Accepts the same C<l=>, C<j=>, C<f=> pipeline params as C</join>, plus:
 7352: 
 7353:   cat=<col>     X-axis category column (required)
 7354:   series=<col>  Stack-segment column (required; seg= is a backwards-compat alias)
 7355:   val=<col>     Numeric measure column (optional; count rows when absent)
 7356:   scheme=<name> D3 colour scheme (default tableau10)
 7357:   back=<url>    URL for the "Back to table" breadcrumb link
 7358: 
 7359: =head3 API SPECIFICATION

 7362: 
 7363:   l       spec    Required.  Left table spec.
 7364:   cat     string  Required.  X-axis categorical column name.
 7365:   series  string  Required.  Stack-segment categorical column name.
 7366:   val     string  Optional.  Numeric measure column; omit for count mode.
 7367:   scheme  string  Optional.  D3 colour scheme name.
 7368:   back    url     Optional.  Safe back-link URL.
 7369: 
 7370: =head4 OUTPUT
 7371: 
 7372:   200 text/html    Rendered stacked bar chart page.

 7377: =head3 FORMAL SPECIFICATION

 7379:   stacked_view == lambda self .
 7380:     pre  length(cat_col) > 0 /\ length(series_col) > 0
 7381:          /\ cat_col in columns /\ series_col in columns
 7382:     let rows = _grouped_totals(records, cat_col, series_col, val_col) in
 7383:     post render(stacked, rows)
 7384: 
 7385: =head3 EXAMPLE

 7425:     post render(combo, pairs)

 7434: =head2 treemap_view
 7435: 
 7436: C<GET /treemap> -- Render a D3.js treemap where tile area encodes a numeric column.
 7437: 
 7438: Accepts the same C<l=>, C<j=>, C<f=> pipeline params as C</join>, plus:
 7439: 
 7440:   group=<col>  Tile-label categorical column (required; cat= is a backwards-compat alias)
 7441:   size=<col>   Tile-area numeric column (required; val= is a backwards-compat alias)
 7442:   topn=N       Keep only the top-N tiles by size; remainder collapsed into "Other"
 7443:   scheme=<name> D3 colour scheme (default tableau10)
 7444:   back=<url>   URL for the "Back to table" breadcrumb link
 7445: 
 7446: =head3 API SPECIFICATION
 7447: 
 7448: =head4 INPUT
 7449: 
 7450:   l       spec    Required.  Left table spec.
 7451:   group   string  Required.  Tile-label categorical column (cat= also accepted).
 7452:   size    string  Required.  Tile-area numeric column (val= also accepted).
 7453:   topn    int     Optional.  Keep top-N tiles; 0 or absent means no limit.
 7454:   scheme  string  Optional.  D3 colour scheme name.
 7455:   back    url     Optional.  Safe back-link URL.
 7456: 
 7457: =head4 OUTPUT
 7458: 
 7459:   200 text/html    Rendered treemap page.
 7460:   400 text/plain   Missing group= or size= parameter.
 7461:   400 text/plain   Unrecognised scheme= parameter (?color= returns 400).
 7462:   400 text/plain   Column not found: <col>.
 7463:   404 text/plain   "Could not open data source".
 7464: 
 7465: =head3 FORMAL SPECIFICATION
 7466: 
 7467:   treemap_view == lambda self .
 7468:     pre  length(group_col) > 0 /\ length(size_col) > 0
 7469:          /\ group_col in columns /\ size_col in columns
 7470:          /\ color param absent (scheme= is the correct param name)
 7471:     let tiles = _grouped_totals(records, group_col, [], size_sum) in
 7472:     let top   = topn > 0 ? top_n(tiles, topn) : tiles in
 7473:     post render(treemap, top)
 7474: 
 7475: =head3 EXAMPLE
 7476: 
 7477:   GET /treemap?l=table:sales&group=product&size=amount&topn=10
 7478:     -> 200 HTML page containing D3 treemap SVG
 7479: 
 7480: =cut
 7481: 
 7482: =head2 histogram_view
 7483: 
 7484: C<GET /histogram> -- Render a D3.js histogram with server-side binning.
 7485: 
 7486: Accepts the same C<l=>, C<j=>, C<f=> pipeline params as C</join>, plus:
 7487: 
 7488:   col=<col>   Numeric column to bin (required)
 7489:   bins=N      Number of bins (default 10; max 200)
 7490:   back=<url>  URL for the "Back to table" breadcrumb link
 7491: 
 7492: =head3 API SPECIFICATION
 7493: 
 7494: =head4 INPUT
 7495: 
 7496:   l     spec    Required.  Left table spec.
 7497:   col   string  Required.  Numeric column to histogram.
 7498:   bins  int     Optional.  Bin count 1-200 (default 10).
 7499:   back  url     Optional.  Safe back-link URL.
 7500: 
 7501: =head4 OUTPUT
 7502: 
 7503:   200 text/html    Rendered histogram page.
 7504:   400 text/plain   Missing col= parameter.
 7505:   400 text/plain   Column not found: <col>.
 7506:   200 text/html    Empty histogram page when no numeric values exist.
 7507:   404 text/plain   "Could not open data source".
 7508: 
 7509: =head3 FORMAL SPECIFICATION
 7510: 
 7511:   histogram_view == lambda self .
 7512:     pre  length(col) > 0 /\ col in columns
 7513:          /\ 1 <= bins <= 200
 7514:     let vals  = { row[col] | row in records, looks_like_number(row[col]) } in
 7515:     let width = (max(vals) - min(vals)) / bins in
 7516:     let bdata = bin(vals, width) in
 7517:     post render(histogram, bdata, bins => |bdata|)
 7518: 
 7519: =head3 EXAMPLE
 7520: 
 7521:   GET /histogram?l=table:sales&col=amount&bins=20
 7522:     -> 200 HTML page containing D3 histogram SVG
 7523: 
 7524: =cut
 7525: 
 7526: =head2 map_view
 7527: 
 7528: C<GET /map> -- Render an OpenStreetMap map of the view.
 7529: 
 7530: A row gets a position one of two ways.  With C<loc=E<lt>columnE<gt>> the column
 7531: holds places, and each distinct value is geocoded once by
 7532: L<Database::BI::Model::Geocode> (one lookup per value however often the column
 7533: repeats it); the answers are remembered in the dataset's model store, so a
 7534: second visit asks nothing.  With C<lat=> and C<lon=> the columns already hold
 7535: coordinates.  Rows sharing a position become one marker whose popup says how
 7536: many rows it stands for.
 7537: 
 7538: Accepts the same C<l=>, C<j=>, C<c=> and C<f=> pipeline params as every other
 7539: visual, so the map shows the filtered, calculated view.
 7540: 
 7541:   loc=<col>      Location column to geocode (required unless lat= and lon= are given)
 7542:   lat=<col>      Latitude column (numeric, -90..90)
 7543:   lon=<col>      Longitude column (numeric, -180..180)
 7544:   popup=<col>    Column shown in the marker popup (repeatable; default: none)
 7545:   max=N          Markers to draw (default: map_max_markers, 2000)
 7546:   back=<url>     URL for the "Back to table" breadcrumb link
 7547: 
 7548: Config values: C<map_geocode_max> (new lookups per request, default 500),
 7549: C<map_max_markers> (markers per request, default 2000), C<map_region> (a
 7550: two-letter country hint for ambiguous place names) and C<geocoder> (an object to
 7551: use instead of loading L<Geo::Coder::Free>; the seam the tests use).
 7552: 
 7553: =head3 API SPECIFICATION
 7554: 
 7555: =head4 INPUT
 7556: 
 7557:   l        spec    Required.  Left table spec (table:name or path:/abs/path).
 7558:   loc      string  Optional.  Column of places to geocode.
 7559:   lat      string  Optional.  Numeric latitude column.
 7560:   lon      string  Optional.  Numeric longitude column.
 7561:   popup    string  Optional, repeatable.  Columns shown in the popup.
 7562:   max      int     Optional.  Positive marker cap for this request.
 7563:   back     url     Optional.  Safe back-link URL (sanitised by _safe_back_url).
 7564: 
 7565: =head4 OUTPUT
 7566: 
 7567:   200 text/html    Rendered map page.
 7568:   400 text/plain   No loc=, or lat= without lon=.
 7569:   400 text/plain   Column not found: <col>  (or the geocode message naming it).
 7570:   400 text/plain   Coordinate column wholly out of range, naming it.
 7571:   404 text/plain   "Could not open data source" when l= is absent/invalid.
 7572: 
 7573: =head3 EXAMPLE
 7574: 
 7575:   GET /map?l=table:sales&loc=place
 7576:     -> 200 HTML page containing Leaflet markers for the places that resolved
 7577: 
 7578: =cut
 7579: 
 7580: =head2 calc_list / calc_save / calc_delete
 7581: 
 7582: C<GET /calc/list>, C<POST /calc/save>, C<POST /calc/delete> -- Manage stored
 7583: calculated fields for a table spec.
 7584: 
 7585: Calculated fields are named expression strings evaluated per-row by
 7586: C<Database::BI::Calc> and appended as virtual columns before any aggregation.
 7587: 
 7588: =head3 API SPECIFICATION
 7589: 
 7590: =head4 calc_list (GET /calc/list)
 7591: 
 7592:   INPUT:  l=<spec>   (required)
 7593:   OUTPUT: 200 application/json  { "fields": { "<name>": "<expr>", ... } }
 7594:           400 application/json  { "error": "l= required" }
 7595: 
 7596: =head4 calc_save (POST /calc/save)
 7597: 
 7598:   INPUT:  l=<spec>   (required)
 7599:           name=<id>  (required; must match /\A[A-Za-z_][A-Za-z0-9_]*\z/)
 7600:           expr=<expression>  (required; max 512 chars; hostile patterns rejected)
 7601:   OUTPUT: 200 application/json  { "saved": 1 }
 7602:           400 application/json  { "error": "..." }  (compile error or hostile input)
 7603: 
 7604: =head4 calc_delete (POST /calc/delete)
 7605: 
 7606:   INPUT:  l=<spec>   (required)
 7607:           name=<id>  (required)
 7608:   OUTPUT: 200 application/json  { "deleted": 1 }
 7609:           400 application/json  { "error": "l= required" }
 7610: 
 7611: =head3 EXAMPLE
 7612: 
 7613:   POST /calc/save  l=path:/data/sales.csv&name=margin&expr=amount-cost
 7614:     -> 200 { "saved": 1 }
 7615:   GET /calc/list?l=path:/data/sales.csv
 7616:     -> 200 { "fields": { "margin": "amount-cost" } }
 7617: 
 7618: =cut
 7619: 
 7620: =head2 groups_list / groups_save / groups_delete
 7621: 
 7622: C<GET /groups/list>, C<POST /groups/save>, C<POST /groups/delete> -- Manage
 7623: named value groups that produce virtual C<${col}_group> columns.
 7624: 
 7625: =head3 API SPECIFICATION
 7626: 
 7627: =head4 groups_list (GET /groups/list)
 7628: 
 7629:   INPUT:  l=<spec>  (required), col=<col>  (required)
 7630:   OUTPUT: 200 application/json  { "groups": { "<label>": ["<val>", ...], ... } }
 7631:           400 application/json  { "error": "l= required" } or "col= required"
 7632: 
 7633: =head4 groups_save (POST /groups/save)
 7634: 
 7635:   INPUT:  l=<spec>, col=<col>, group=<label>, values=<newline-separated values>
 7636:   OUTPUT: 200 application/json  { "saved": 1 }
 7637:           400 application/json  { "error": "..." }
 7638: 
 7639: =head4 groups_delete (POST /groups/delete)
 7640: 
 7641:   INPUT:  l=<spec>, col=<col>, group=<label>  (omit group= to delete all groups for col)
 7642:   OUTPUT: 200 application/json  { "deleted": 1 }
 7643:           400 application/json  { "error": "l= required" }
 7644: 
 7645: =head3 EXAMPLE
 7646: 
 7647:   POST /groups/save  l=path:/data/sales.csv&col=region&group=North&values=MN%0AWI%0AIA
 7648:     -> 200 { "saved": 1 }
 7649:   GET /groups/list?l=path:/data/sales.csv&col=region
 7650:     -> 200 { "groups": { "North": ["MN","WI","IA"] } }
 7651: 
 7652: =cut
 7653: 
 7654: =head2 dates_list / dates_save / dates_delete
 7655: 
 7656: C<GET /dates/list>, C<POST /dates/save>, C<POST /dates/delete> -- Manage stored
 7657: date-normalisation patterns that convert non-ISO date columns to ISO-8601
 7658: (C<YYYY-MM-DD>) in-place before aggregation.
 7659: 
 7660: =head3 API SPECIFICATION
 7661: 
 7662: =head4 dates_list (GET /dates/list)
 7663: 
 7664:   INPUT:  l=<spec>  (required)
 7665:   OUTPUT: 200 application/json  { "patterns": { "<col>": "<pattern>", ... } }
 7666:           400 application/json  { "error": "l= required" }
 7667: 
 7668: =head4 dates_save (POST /dates/save)
 7669: 
 7670:   INPUT:  l=<spec>, col=<col>, pattern=<fmt>
 7671:           Supported patterns: %m/%d/%Y  %d/%m/%Y  %d-%b-%Y
 7672:   OUTPUT: 200 application/json  { "ok": 1 }
 7673:           400 application/json  { "error": "Unsupported date format pattern ..." }
 7674: 
 7675: =head4 dates_delete (POST /dates/delete)
 7676: 
 7677:   INPUT:  l=<spec>, col=<col>  (omit col= to delete all patterns for the spec)
 7678:   OUTPUT: 200 application/json  { "deleted": 1 }
 7679:           400 application/json  { "error": "l= required" }
 7680: 
 7681: =head3 EXAMPLE
 7682: 
 7683:   POST /dates/save  l=path:/data/sales.csv&col=sale_date&pattern=%m/%d/%Y
 7684:     -> 200 { "ok": 1 }
 7685:   GET /dates/list?l=path:/data/sales.csv
 7686:     -> 200 { "patterns": { "sale_date": "%m/%d/%Y" } }
 7687: 
 7688: =cut
 7689: 
 7690: =head1 DESCRIPTION
 7691: 
 7692: All user-facing routes in C<Database::BI> are handled by this controller.
 7693: See the individual action POD above for per-endpoint documentation.
 7694: 
 7695: =head2 Filter operators and the quick-filter bar
 7696: 
 7697: Filters are expressed as C<f=col:op:val> query parameters and are applied
 7698: server-side after all joins.  Multiple C<f=> params are applied in order
 7699: (AND semantics).
 7700: 
 7701: The colon separator is split with a limit of 3, so values may themselves
 7702: contain colons (e.g. C<f=sale_date:eq:2025-01-15>, or a time value like
 7703: C<f=start_time:eq:14:30:00>).
 7704: 
 7705: B<Supported operators:>
 7706: 
 7707:   eq        case-insensitive string equality
 7708:   ne        case-insensitive string inequality
 7709:   contains  case-insensitive substring match
 7710:   starts    case-insensitive prefix match
 7711:   lt        numeric less-than
 7712:   le        numeric less-than-or-equal
 7713:   gt        numeric greater-than
 7714:   ge        numeric greater-than-or-equal
 7715:   empty     cell is undef or empty string (val ignored)
 7716:   notempty  cell is defined and non-empty (val ignored)
 7717: 
 7718: B<Quick-filter bar (table view):>
 7719: 
 7720: A filter row below the toolbar lets the user pick a column, operator, and
 7721: value and press "Apply filter" (or Enter) to add a condition without
 7722: opening any panel.  The current URL is updated with the new C<f=> param
 7723: and the page reloads; all other URL parameters (joins, dedup, etc.) are
 7724: preserved automatically via the browser C<URL> API.
 7725: 
 7726: Active filter chips appear in the toolbar.  Each chip has an individual
 7727: x-button to remove just that condition.  The "x Clear filters" link
 7728: removes all C<f=> params at once.
 7729: 
 7730: B<Drill-down from pie chart:>
 7731: 
 7732: Clicking a pie slice or legend entry on C</pie> navigates to the table
 7733: view with C<f=cat_col:eq:label> appended, so the user can inspect the
 7734: rows behind any slice without manually typing a filter.
 7735: 
 7736: =head1 COMMON PITFALLS
 7737: 
 7738: These are the most common mistakes when working with this controller.
 7739: 
 7740: =over 4
 7741: 
 7742: =item B<SQLite files must use the .sql extension, not .sqlite>
 7743: 
 7744: C<Database::Abstraction> (the data-reading library) probes for a file called
 7745: C<tablename.sql> when it wants to open a SQLite database.  It does B<not>
 7746: look for C<.sqlite> or C<.db3>.  If your file is called C<inventory.sqlite>,
 7747: rename it to C<inventory.sql> or it will not appear in the file browser and
 7748: will return 404 when opened.
 7749: 
 7750: =item B<Filter values that contain a colon still work>
 7751: 
 7752: A date like C<2025-01-15> contains hyphens, not colons, so it is fine.  But
 7753: if your value itself contains a colon (for example, a time like C<14:30:00>),
 7754: the filter still works because the C<col:op:val> spec is split on the B<first
 7755: two> colons only -- the rest of the string becomes the value.
 7756: 
 7757:   # This correctly matches "14:30:00" in the start_time column:
 7758:   ?f=start_time:eq:14:30:00
 7759: 
 7760: =item B<Left join keeps only the FIRST matching right-table row>
 7761: 
 7762: When the right table has two rows with the same join key, only the first one
 7763: (in file order) is used.  The second is silently ignored.  If you need all
 7764: matches, consider pre-processing your data so join keys are unique.
 7765: 
 7766: =item B<Export format comes from the filename extension, not a Content-Type header>
 7767: 
 7768: When using C<POST /export> to save a file to disk, the format (CSV or SQLite)
 7769: is determined by the extension of the C<filename> parameter.  C<.csv> produces
 7770: a CSV file; C<.sql> produces a SQLite database.  Any other extension returns
 7771: HTTP 415 (Unsupported Media Type).  The C<Content-Type> request header is
 7772: ignored entirely.
 7773: 
 7774: =item B<Template Toolkit variables starting with underscore are silently dropped>
 7775: 
 7776: If you add a stash variable with a name starting with C<_> (for example,
 7777: C<_tmp> or C<_result>), Template Toolkit will silently produce an empty string
 7778: when the template tries to read it.  This is a TT quirk when C<TRIM =E<gt> 1>
 7779: is active.  Always use names that start with a letter.
 7780: 
 7781: =item B<_apply_filter_spec always passes all records through for unknown operators>
 7782: 
 7783: If you pass an operator that is not in the supported list (for example C<regex>
 7784: or C<like>), the filter is treated as a no-op and B<all rows are returned>.  No
 7785: error is produced.  This is intentional so that future operators can be added
 7786: without breaking existing clients that read a wider response.
 7787: 
 7788: =item B<Uploaded files are evicted automatically, but not immediately>
 7789: 
 7790: Files uploaded via C<POST /upload> are stored in C<.uploads/> under the
 7791: application home directory.  C<Database::BI> evicts upload subdirectories
 7792: whose modification time is older than 24 hours B<on every server startup>.
 7793: Between restarts, files accumulate.  For an immediate full purge (regardless
 7794: of age), post to C<POST /uploads/clear> or click the "Clear upload cache"
 7795: button in the UI.
 7796: 
 7797: =item B<Open C<data/> tables by name; open other files by absolute path>
 7798: 
 7799: The C<view> action (C<GET /view/:table>) only looks inside C<data_dir>.
 7800: To open a file from anywhere else on the filesystem, use C<open_file>
 7801: (C<GET /open?path=/abs/path>).  The two routes use different URL schemes and
 7802: are not interchangeable.
 7803: 
 7804: =back
 7805: 
 7806: =head1 LIMITATIONS
 7807: 
 7808: =over 4
 7809: 
 7810: =item *
 7811: 
 7812: Multi-table joins are delegated to C<Database::Join> with C<backend =E<gt> 'auto'>.
 7813: For datasets up to C<join_max_rows> combined rows (default 10,000; configurable
 7814: in C<database_bi.conf>) the join runs in Perl memory; larger datasets spill to
 7815: a temporary SQLite database so peak RAM is bounded by the result set rather than
 7816: the sum of all source tables.
 7817: 
 7818: =item *
 7819: 
 7820: C<_resolve_language> extracts only the primary language subtag from the first
 7821: C<Accept-Language> tag (e.g. C<de> from C<de-DE,de;q=0.9,en;q=0.8>).
 7822: Quality weights and multiple alternatives are not ranked.
 7823: 
 7824: =item *
 7825: 
 7826: No GeoIP-based language resolution is implemented.  The language is resolved
 7827: from the HTTP C<Accept-Language> header only.
 7828: 
 7829: =item *
 7830: 
 7831: C<Sub::Protected> enforcement requires the CHECK compilation phase; when modules
 7832: are loaded dynamically at test time the "Too late to run CHECK block" warning
 7833: is emitted and the restriction is not enforced in that context.
 7834: 
 7835: =back
